# Historical session knowledge and Skill audit

Status: proposal only. Audit date: 2026-08-12. Historical cutoff: `2026-08-12T02:12:21.718Z`.

No new Skill passes the worth-it gate. The recurring gbdraw workflows are already covered by repository Skills, global Skills, read-only specialist agents, engineering contracts, and executable checks. One existing Skill, `maintain-python-api`, has drifted from the current documentation ownership model and should be updated after review. Two CLAUDE pointers also need correction: Gallery generation in the Web guide and CLI documentation ownership in the root guide. The remaining gaps belong in Web architecture documentation, an internal-plan index, contributor documentation, CI or PR evidence, and the finite release plans already in the repository.

This audit did not edit a Skill, policy file, source file, test, workflow, or original session. The only repository change is this report.

## A. Corpus coverage and limitations

### Sources

| Source | Discovery result | Included in evidence | Limitation |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Requested Codex Markdown export | No directory matching the placeholder or a rollout-style Markdown corpus was found | 0 | The requested export path was not supplied and no local substitute in Markdown form exists. |
| Requested ChatGPT export | No standard `conversations.json`, `chat.html`, `shared_conversations.json`, or matching archive was found in the searched home, Downloads, Documents, Desktop, OneDrive, repository, or `/tmp` locations | 0 | None of 322 ZIP files in the searched common locations contained `conversations.json`. ChatGPT history is therefore out of scope. |
| Native Codex active store | 1,155 JSONL files overall; 788 files had first-record metadata rooted at gbdraw at scan time | 784 historical files | Four audit-time sessions began at or after the cutoff and were excluded. |
| Native Codex archive | 1,005 JSONL files overall; 892 files had first-record metadata rooted at gbdraw | 892 historical files | This is raw Codex JSONL, not the requested Markdown export. |
| Current repository | Working tree, five repository-local Skills, relevant global Skills and plugin trigger descriptions, instructions, docs, scripts, tests, workflows, and local git history | Included | GitHub CLI had no authentication. Direct issue and PR bodies were not available; local merge commits, plan references, and explicit session references were used when recoverable. |
| Prior audit from 2026-08-04 | Existing report artifact under `reports/codex-session-audit-2026-08-04/` | Corroboration only | It is a derived report, not independent transcript evidence. |

The frozen set contains 1,676 historical session files, 5,442,227,621 bytes (5.07 GiB), dated 2026-01-13 through 2026-08-12. All selected files parsed as UTF-8 JSONL without malformed lines. A stat-before/stat-after comparison found no size or modification-time change in any selected source file during extraction.

### Canonicalization and lineage

- Repository membership and canonical session ID come from the first `session_meta` record. This matters because 322 active-store files contain more than one metadata record.
- Slash direction and path case were normalized for repository matching. Starting `cwd` establishes where a session began; it does not prove that every task in that session concerned gbdraw.
- The 1,676 sessions comprise 1,000 top-level root sessions, 530 explicit spawned-agent sessions, 137 guardian sessions, and 9 internal exec sessions. Only top-level root arcs contribute to recurrence counts.
- Parent linkage is incomplete in older subagent schemas. Auxiliary sessions remain in the inventory but are not independent recurrence evidence.
- Raw segmentation produced 5,167 arcs across all lineages. The 1,000 root sessions yielded 2,749 root arcs. Nineteen userless automatic-continuation fragments were merged into their preceding goal, leaving 2,730 normalized root arcs.
- Newer records were split on explicit task boundaries. Older records without task IDs were split on user-message boundaries; 164 root sessions still carry the `S` limitation in the appendix because exact turn ownership of every tool action is less certain.
- There were 310 context-compaction markers in 201 normalized root arcs. A compaction was retained inside the same arc when the user goal did not change.

### Evidence fields and outcome semantics

The extractor retained bounded, redacted task summaries, path keys, explicit issue or PR number strings, result commit IDs when a commit result was recoverable, test command class and exit status, lifecycle events, and byte counts. It did not retain reasoning text, tool-output bodies, code blocks, credentials, uploaded file bodies, or full messages.

Changed-path evidence was recoverable in 1,085 sessions and explicit issue or PR-like references in 40. No result commit survived repository-object validation; seven numeric strings initially matched the permissive legacy pattern and were rejected. The per-session appendix shows at most four exact normalized paths and records the full recovered count. This bound avoids turning a proposal report into a 44,000-entry path dump; it is also appropriate because path extraction from old shell output is evidence, not a perfect diff reconstruction.

For the 1,000 root sessions, lifecycle classification found 719 task-complete events, 172 abort events, 87 failure-or-follow-up markers, and 22 sessions without a reliable closing signal. A task-complete event is not proof that the implementation was correct or merged. No automatic outcome label is treated as verified success, and no reliable automatic revert signal was recovered.

### Current repository comparison

The repository currently has five local Skills, 116 Python test files, 65 Node test files, 12 Playwright specifications, and 16 tracked reference SVGs. Recent commits corroborate several transcript findings:

- `20488c00` separates ordinary history edits from artifact-heavy checkpoints.
- `112544a5` unifies sanitized SVG result ingestion.
- `366aeac2` activates Web performance guardrails.
- `32007290` aligns Web CI gates with current owners.

The 2026-08-04 audit recommended plan execution, cross-surface rendering, browser/offline QA, release preparation, plan criticism, and visual review workflows. Those capabilities now exist as Skills or read-only specialist agents. That earlier report supports the current-state comparison but was not counted as a second historical arc.

## B. Repeated friction clusters

Counts below are multi-label signatures over 2,730 normalized top-level arcs. They overlap and should not be added. Directional signatures are used to find clusters, not to estimate engineering effort.

| Cluster | Recurrence | Repeated failure or correction pattern | Current state |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Named-plan execution and review | 257 arcs / 226 sessions for execution; 119 / 105 for review; 38 sessions explicitly requested five plan defects | Plans were implemented before assumptions, surface coverage, or test ownership were settled, followed by plan revisions or user corrections | Covered by `execute-plan-with-evidence` and the read-only `plan_critic` agent |
| Cross-surface rendering changes | 350 / 304 | A setting or track existed in one of CLI, Web, Python, session replay, or the renderer but not the others; a narrow track fix often left sibling track modes unchanged | Covered well by `change-gbdraw-rendering-surface`; contract and regression tests now provide stronger enforcement |
| Web intent, artifacts, and history | 11 / 9 explicit Undo/Redo arcs; several produced follow-up regressions | Undo/Redo restored stale generated artifacts, lost selection/editability, or copied too much SVG state; ordinary edits became slow | Code and tests now distinguish intent from artifact checkpoints, but `gbdraw/web/CLAUDE.md` does not yet state that boundary |
| Session compatibility and replay | 108 / 94 directional; 56 / 49 explicit | Web save/load, CLI handoff, cache identity, current writer, old readers, and regenerated Gallery sessions were repeatedly conflated | Strong current owners exist in `docs/SESSION_COMPATIBILITY.md`, the reference page, fixtures, Python tests, and Web tests |
| Offline browser packaging | 76 / 56 explicit | Stale or missing browser wheels, recursive wheel inclusion, deploy preparation mismatch, CSP/vendor issues, and Pyodide readiness regressions recurred | Covered by `browser-offline-qa`, preparation and verification scripts, packaging tests, and CI |
| Reproducible documentation and Gallery truth | 402 / 322 directional; 147 / 122 explicit documentation requests; 18 direct Gallery/tutorial correction sessions | Screenshots used the wrong mode or state, captions did not match the crop, public figures lost useful context, or generated sessions drifted from their recipe | Covered by `love-me-love-my-docs` and `web-gallery-screenshot-maintenance`; one Web guide pointer is stale |
| Public Python API contract | 121 / 103 | Public forwarding, strict error semantics, sessions, examples, and CLI/Web parity were repeatedly audited or repaired | Covered by `maintain-python-api`, but that Skill still treats a legacy router as the documentation owner |
| Performance evidence | 138 / 114 directional; 92 / 72 explicit | Perceived slowness led to diagnosis after the change; browser history and large-session paths lacked base/head evidence or durable budgets | Guardrails and a benchmark runner exist; the standard 3-warmup/21-sample comparison is not a CI lane and there is no PR evidence template |
| Generated-artifact ownership | 70 / 65 | Browser wheels, Gallery sessions, screenshots, examples, notices, and reference output were regenerated ad hoc or by the wrong owner | Deterministic scripts and focused tests now own most operations; use them rather than a general regeneration Skill |
| Visual geometry review | 327 / 222 directional; 98 / 77 direct spacing, overlap, or placement corrections | A local visual fix moved the wrong boundary, fixed one mode only, or produced unreadable public examples | Covered by rendering and Gallery Skills plus the read-only visual reviewer |
| CI failure diagnosis | 33 / 27 direct failure arcs | Packaging and browser failures were often treated as isolated test errors before owner, generated input, or workflow ordering was traced | Covered by the GitHub `gh-fix-ci` workflow and repository test ownership; no gbdraw-specific Skill is warranted |
| Architecture convergence and scope contamination | 116 / 101 convergence arcs; 216 / 170 directional correction arcs | Agents introduced parallel editors, adapters, or partial fixes, then users redirected the work to the shared owner or another surface | Root and Web architecture guides already state single-owner rules; focused contract tests are the enforcement point |

### C.2 Trigger, benefit, and risk

| ID | Proposed trigger and exclusions, if a Skill | Expected benefit | Main risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Keep the current trigger. Exclude internal refactors that do not change public behavior, persistence, topology, or output. | Prevent one-surface and one-mode completion claims. | Over-triggering on renderer-internal cleanup. |
| C2 | Keep the current trigger for a named plan, checklist, roadmap, or phased migration. Exclude ordinary single-change requests without a plan artifact. | Truthful status and evidence tied to plan gates. | Treating stale plan text as authority without repository reconciliation; the Skill already guards against this. |
| C3 | Not a Skill proposal. | Independent criticism before execution. | Mechanical requests for a fixed issue count can reward minor findings; the agent must rank material issues. |
| C4 | Keep the current documentation trigger. Exclude small prose-only fixes that need no reproduction evidence. | Published instructions and visible output share a reproducible source. | Capture cost can be disproportionate for low-risk text changes. |
| C5 | Keep the current Gallery-specific trigger. Exclude general app UI work with no Gallery media or tutorial impact. | Prevent wrong-state screenshots and stale session/result metadata. | Large capture refreshes can hide unrelated visual changes. |
| C6 | Keep the current offline/static-browser trigger. Exclude server-backed browser work whose runtime network access is intentional. | Catch wheel, CSP, vendoring, lifecycle, and cache failures before deploy. | A local ready-state check alone may miss hosted-path behavior. |
| C7 | Keep the current API trigger. Exclude private implementation helpers with no public contract, docs, or session impact. | Restore correct documentation routing while preserving its strong contract workflow. | Copying current docs structure into the Skill too deeply would create another drift point. |
| C8 | Not a Skill proposal. | Make the mutation classification discoverable before agents edit history-sensitive Web code. | A prose-only rule can drift unless it points to focused tests and owners. |
| C9 | Not a new Skill. Existing C1/C7 triggers cover changes that cross this boundary. | Preserve an evidence-based writer/reader matrix without another workflow layer. | Supporting branch-only versions or treating cache fields as schema identity. |
| C10 | Not a Skill proposal. Apply the template only when touched paths or the author identify performance sensitivity; exclude docs-only and generated-text-only changes. | Move performance evidence before merge rather than after user-visible regression. | Template ritual without a stable runner; require exact command, base/head revisions, and result artifact. |
| C11 | Not a Skill proposal. | Deterministic, reviewable regeneration through existing owners. | A generic wrapper would hide artifact-specific preconditions and review rules. |
| C12 | Not a Skill proposal. | Let agents select the current plan and detect superseded or completed work before execution. | The index becomes stale unless each plan change updates status metadata. |
| C13 | Keep the current release trigger. Exclude publishing, tagging, pushing, and deployment unless explicitly authorized. | One build-once, inspect-before-publish workflow across package and hosted artifacts. | Referencing Work Package H outputs before they exist. |
| C14 | Not a Skill proposal. | Separate visual judgment from implementation and catch wrong-boundary fixes. | Visual review without exact reproduction evidence can approve a non-reproducible image. |
| C15 | Keep the plugin trigger for failing GitHub Actions checks. Exclude local-only test diagnosis without a PR or check context. | Reuse check/log inspection instead of a repository-specific duplicate. | GitHub authentication or remote log availability may block it. |
| C16 | Not a Skill proposal. | Restore one truthful generated Gallery owner pointer. | Naming two scripts without explaining their separate roles would preserve ambiguity. |
| C17 | Not a Skill proposal. | Contributor instructions match executable CI ownership. | Repeating the full workflow matrix in prose would drift again; link to the workflow and summarize roles. |
| C18 | Not a Skill proposal. Run on a stable scheduled or release lane rather than every low-risk PR unless runtime proves acceptable. | Detect drift across browser screenshots and executable documentation as a whole. | Runtime, browser variance, and large generated diffs. |
| C19 | Not a Skill proposal. | Send agents to the public behavior owner while retaining the generated help inventory for exact option-set checks. | Removing the legacy pointer instead of relabeling it could make the generated CLI inventory undiscoverable. |

## D. Rejected Skill candidates

| Rejected candidate | Why it failed the worth-it gate | Better destination |
|---|---|---|
| `maintain-gbdraw-web-editor-state` | The durable content is an architectural invariant and mutation classification. It is better stated once in the Web contract and enforced by history, ingestion, and browser tests. It also overlaps C1, C6, and C9. | `gbdraw/web/CLAUDE.md` plus existing focused tests |
| `maintain-session-compatibility` | Current compatibility docs, fixtures, adapters, and cross-surface/API Skills already cover the procedure. A new Skill would repeat version policy and likely drift. | Existing compatibility contract and tests |
| `verify-gbdraw-performance` | Measurement is deterministic work already owned by benchmark and Playwright code. The remaining gap is required evidence at review time, not procedural knowledge unavailable to a capable agent. | PR template and existing runners |
| `regenerate-gbdraw-artifacts` | Browser wheels, Gallery sessions, screenshots, examples, notices, and reference SVGs have different owners and safety rules. One umbrella Skill would hide those distinctions. | Existing scripts, contract tests, C4/C5/C6 Skills |
| `review-gbdraw-implementation-plan` | The trigger and method duplicate the global plan workflow and read-only plan critic. | Existing Skill and agent |
| `triage-gbdraw-ci` | The GitHub plugin already owns remote check and log inspection; local details belong in tests and workflow definitions. | `github:gh-fix-ci` |
| `prepare-gbdraw-release` | The global bioinformatics release Skill already supports a concise project reference. Current gbdraw release work is a finite Work Package H campaign. | Existing release Skill and temporary execution plan |
| `converge-gbdraw-architecture` | Single-owner and no-parallel-path rules are permanent architecture, not a bounded recurring task workflow. | Root/Web architecture guides and contract tests |

No rejected candidate is rescued by a single high-impact session. Each either duplicates a current capability or is more reliably enforced by a contract, script, test, CI check, or finite plan.

## E. Proposed updates to existing Skills

### E1. Update `maintain-python-api`

Priority: High. Trigger: unchanged.

The Skill's implementation guidance is strong, but its documentation section reflects the former documentation model:

- It tells agents to inspect and add recipes to `docs/PYTHON_API.md`. That file is now a short link-preserving router.
- It says `tests/test_api_library_usage.py` executes every Python code block sequentially in one namespace. The current executable owner is `docs/recipes/run_python_scenarios.py`, driven by `docs/scenarios/manifest.json`, and each recipe runs in a clean temporary directory. `tests/test_documented_recipes.py` explicitly requires the legacy router to contain no runnable blocks.
- Exact public behavior now belongs in `docs/REFERENCE/python-api.md`; learning recipes belong in the manifest-selected Python Tutorial destinations. `docs/REFERENCE/command-line.md` is the public CLI behavior owner, while `docs/CLI_Reference.md` remains a generated/help inventory where applicable.

Proposed edit after approval:

1. Replace legacy documentation owners with `docs/REFERENCE/python-api.md`, `docs/scenarios/manifest.json`, and the manifest-selected Tutorial destination.
2. Route executable-example checks to `docs/recipes/run_python_scenarios.py` and its contract tests.
3. Keep the existing public-contract, forwarding, session, error, and reference-output guidance unchanged.
4. Keep the change concise; point to current owners instead of copying their procedures into the Skill.

### Existing Skills that do not need an update

- `change-gbdraw-rendering-surface` already maps typed core, Python, CLI, Web, persistence, rendering, documentation, tests, and generated evidence. Once C8 is documented in the Web guide, its existing instruction to read that guide is sufficient.
- `love-me-love-my-docs` already requires clean execution, source-data provenance, real capture, visible-result review, and regeneration evidence.
- `web-gallery-screenshot-maintenance` already contains the non-obvious state, crop, popup, session metadata, and same-size review rules found in the direct correction arcs.
- `execute-plan-with-evidence`, `browser-offline-qa`, and `prepare-bioinformatics-release` match their recurring classes without a gbdraw-specific fork.

## F. Proposed new Skills, ordered by priority

None.

The correct priority order is therefore empty. Reconsider a new Skill only after a current owner has been corrected and the same workflow still fails to trigger in at least two independent future arcs. In particular, do not create a Web-state Skill merely to restate C8, and do not create a release Skill for Work Package H milestones.

## H. Transcript privacy and prompt-injection concerns

- Every transcript field was treated as historical data. Prompt-like user, assistant, developer, Skill, plugin, issue, source-file, and tool-output text was never treated as an active instruction.
- The extractor skipped developer/environment blocks, reasoning bodies, and tool-output bodies. It used tool records only for bounded metadata such as command class, exit code, changed-path key, and commit result.
- Bounded summaries redact secret-like tokens, bearer values, credential assignments, email addresses, URLs, IP addresses, home/user paths, and fenced code. The report does not reproduce a credential or private URL. This was a privacy-preserving knowledge audit, not a credential leak scan.
- Full uploaded files, source payloads, shell output, and transcript messages are absent. Appendix summaries are capped and sanitized; paths are bounded to four examples per session.
- Repeated `session_meta` records were a material parser hazard. The first record, not the last, defines canonical ID, lineage, and repository membership. Using the last record would have collapsed spawned-agent files into parent identities and understated the corpus.
- Four audit-time sessions were excluded by the frozen cutoff. Selected files were stat-checked before and after extraction and were unchanged.
- Keyword clusters can over-include synonyms and undercount novel wording. Direct-evidence counts are shown beside broad directional counts where that distinction affects a decision.
- Direct GitHub issue and PR body review was unavailable without authentication. The report does not infer thread resolution state from a bare number; related references in the appendix are lexical evidence only.

## Appendix 1. Per-session inventory

This appendix records every selected session. It is intentionally compact.

Column conventions:

- `Context`: `root`, `subagent`, `guardian`, or `exec`, followed by active/archive storage. All rows started with normalized `cwd` equal to the gbdraw repository.
- `Paths`: up to four exact normalized recovered paths, followed by the total when more were found.
- `Refs`: `base` is the repository revision at session start, not a result commit. `#N` is an explicit issue/PR-like reference whose type may be unknown. A `result` would require a commit recovered from a commit result and validated as a repository object; none survived that check in this corpus.
- `Outcome`: lifecycle evidence only: `complete-event`, `aborted`, `failure/follow-up`, `reported-blocked`, `monitor`, or `unknown`.
- `Limits`: `N` no thread title; `X` bounded message truncation; `S` old schema without explicit turn IDs; `T` task summary unavailable because the bounded record held only interruption, command, or runtime boilerplate; `A` auxiliary lineage excluded from recurrence; `P` not all recovered paths are displayed; `O` outcome is event-derived and not verified success.
